## Verdict

**Verified — the fix is correct and safe to merge.** All applicable checks pass. One finding worth relaying to the author before merge: the three new tests do not fully pin down the fix (a wrong-but-plausible mutation still passes them), and there is one real path in the repo whose CI routing genuinely changes as a side effect of this fix (not a bug in the fix itself, but a pre-existing gap it unmasks).

## Independent check of the issue-#107 dispute

The user's correction is right; the issue's "benign / over-matches into the safe direction" characterization is wrong. Verified two ways:

1. **Analytically**: pre-fix, `path.startswith(pattern[:-3])` strips the pattern's separator (e.g. `"docs/**"[:-3] == "docs"`, not `"docs/"`), so any sibling whose name merely starts with the directory name matches — `"docs-archive/notes.md".startswith("docs")` is `True`. Post-fix, `pattern[:-2]` keeps the separator (`"docs/**"[:-2] == "docs/"`), so the same path no longer matches.
2. **Empirically**, running `classify`/`recommended_targets` from both the pre-fix version (`git show d8fd6ff:scripts/detect_changed_surfaces.py`) and the working-tree fix against the three example paths, I reproduced the user's numbers exactly:
   - `docs-archive/notes.md` → pre: `['docs-check']` only; post: full runtime suite.
   - `apps/web-e2e/spec.ts` → pre: `['quick-ci-web']` only; post: full runtime suite.
   - `services/chat-legacy/old.py` → pre: `['quick-ci-chat']` only; post: full runtime suite.

`unknown` drives the runtime fallback (`classify()`: `if unknown: runtime = True`), and a spurious pattern match is exactly what keeps `unknown` False. So the pre-fix bug does not "over-match into the safe direction" — it suppresses the one mechanism (`unknown → runtime`) that exists for safety, and narrows CI to a single scoped job. `docs/**` in `DOC_PATTERNS` does allow a docs-sibling directory to fully skip the runtime suite pre-fix, confirmed above. The user's claim is correct and the issue text is wrong.

## Does the fix break routing for any path currently in the repo?

Ran `classify`/`recommended_targets` (pre-fix vs. post-fix) over:
- every file in every one of the repo's 362 commits (`git show --name-only &lt;commit&gt;` per commit) — **zero differences**.
- every file currently tracked in the repo (`git ls-files`, 1344 files) — **one difference**: `apps/web/prisma.config.ts` goes from `['quick-ci-web', 'quick-ci-chat']` (pre-fix, via the same separator bug matching `apps/web/prisma/**`) to `['quick-ci-web']` (post-fix, correctly excluded since it's a sibling file, not inside `apps/web/prisma/`).

This one case is worth flagging: `Dockerfile.migrate` explicitly `COPY`s `apps/web/prisma.config.ts` (`/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/contoso/Dockerfile.migrate`), and `CHAT_PATTERNS` in `scripts/detect_changed_surfaces.py` already deliberately lists `apps/web/prisma/**` and `Dockerfile.migrate` as chat-relevant. `apps/web/prisma.config.ts` was accidentally covered only because of the bug being fixed here; `CHAT_PATTERNS` never explicitly listed it. After this fix, editing `apps/web/prisma.config.ts` alone will route to `quick-ci-web` only, not `quick-ci-chat`, even though it configures the shared Prisma schema/migration path that the chat-related migrate image consumes. This is not a defect introduced by the diff under review — the diff is a correct, narrowly-scoped bug fix — but it is a real, pre-existing coverage gap in `CHAT_PATTERNS` that this fix unmasks rather than causes. Worth a follow-up line (`"apps/web/prisma.config.ts"` added to `CHAT_PATTERNS`).

## Are the three new tests meaningful?

Partially. I mutation-tested by temporarily replacing the fixed line with a substring-containment check (`return pattern[:-2] in path` instead of `path.startswith(...)`) and reran only `tests/scripts/test_detect_changed_surfaces.py`:

```
return path.startswith(pattern[:-2])   # fixed, correct
return (pattern[:-2] in path)          # mutation
```

All 14 tests in that file, including all three new ones, **passed** against the wrong mutation. Confirmed the practical consequence: with the mutation, `path_matches("docs/**", "vendor/docs/old.md")` and `path_matches("apps/web/**", "backup/apps/web/foo.ts")` both wrongly return `True`, and `classify(["vendor/docs/old.md"])` wrongly reports `docs=True, runtime=False` — i.e. a nested/backed-up path anywhere containing `dir/` as a substring would be misrouted, potentially suppressing the runtime fallback the same way the original bug did. None of the four `(pattern, sibling)` / `(pattern, child)` pairs in the new tests probe a path where the pattern's directory appears anywhere other than position 0, so a substring check (rather than a true prefix check) is indistinguishable from the correct fix under this suite. I restored the file to its original diff content afterward and reran the suite to confirm no residual changes (`diff` against a backup was byte-identical, `git status --short` shows only the original two modified files).

So: the tests correctly kill the specific regression the issue describes (missing separator) but are not exhaustive against nearby wrong implementations (e.g., "contains" vs. "starts with"). This is a real but minor coverage gap — worth a one-line addition such as `path_matches("docs/**", "vendor/docs/notes.md")` asserting `False`.

## Other `/**`/prefix-matching logic in the repo with the same defect

Searched all `startswith`/`startsWith`/`.match(`/glob usage in `scripts/`, `services/chat`, `apps/web/src`, and workflow-adjacent tooling. Found no other instance of the same missing-separator defect:
- `apps/web/src/lib/messaging.ts` uses `PRODUCTS_PATH_PREFIX = "/products/"` with the trailing slash already included — safe.
- All other `startswith` usages (`scripts/check_env_contract.py`, `scripts/check_agent_docs.py`, `scripts/verify_docs.py`, `services/chat/scripts/check_dependency_policy.py`) match comment markers, bullet prefixes, or URL schemes, not directory containment — not the same class of bug.
- No other `dir/**`-style pattern-matching function exists elsewhere in the repo; `path_matches` in `scripts/detect_changed_surfaces.py` is the only one.

## Verification of the two round-1 fixes

**Finding A — `apps/web/prisma.config.ts` in `CHAT_PATTERNS`: correct fix, and provably the only one needed.**

`/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/contoso/Dockerfile.migrate` does `COPY apps/web/prisma.config.ts ./apps/web/prisma.config.ts`, so the coupling the comment claims is real. More importantly, I checked whether a broader pattern was warranted by classifying every possible *shape* of sibling against old vs. new `path_matches`, not just tracked files. The result generalizes cleanly:

- For every glob except `apps/web/prisma/**`, a lost sibling match falls through to `unknown`, and `unknown` forces `runtime`, which is a strict superset of the lost surface. E.g. `services/chat-legacy/x.py` goes `chat` → `runtime,unknown`. Safe direction, no coverage lost.
- `apps/web/prisma/**` is the sole exception, because `apps/web/**` keeps such paths non-`unknown` and therefore suppresses the fallback. That is exactly the `prisma.config.ts` case, and it is the *only* silent-loss family in the repo.

So a targeted literal entry is the right shape rather than broadening the glob. The residual hypothetical — a future `apps/web/prisma.seed.ts` or `prisma.config.mts` would silently lose chat the same way — is not a defect in this change (I scored it ~40 and am not reporting it as a finding); I mention it only because you asked directly. If you want belt-and-braces, `"apps/web/prisma*"` matches via `Path.match` today and would cover the family, at the cost of also catching a hypothetical `prismafoo.ts`.

I found no generated, gitignored, or untracked-but-CI-relevant path affected. The detector only ever sees `git diff --name-only` output or `git status --porcelain` output, so ignored paths cannot reach it, and untracked directories arrive with a trailing `/` (`docs/`), which the new prefix still matches.

**Finding B — anchoring test: genuinely load-bearing.** I built a mutation harness (18 mutants) and confirmed per-mutant which test kills it:

| mutant | killed by pre-existing tests? | killed by new tests |
|---|---|---|
| `pattern[:-3]` (the original bug) | no | sibling test, CI-narrowing test |
| `pattern[:-2] in path` (substring) | no | **only** `test_directory_glob_anchors_at_the_start_of_the_path` |
| drop `apps/web/prisma.config.ts` | no | **only** `test_prisma_config_stays_in_the_chat_surface` |
| require deeper nesting | yes | children test, prisma test |

Also killed: `pattern[:-1]`, `pattern[:-4]`, `True`, `False`, `endswith`, `pattern[:-3] in path`, component-split matching, dash-sibling and dot-sibling allowances, deleting the glob branch, and removing `if unknown: runtime = True`. **No surviving wrong implementation of `path_matches`.** Three mutants survived — dropping `apps/web/prisma/**`, `services/chat/**`, or `docs/**` from their lists — but those are pre-existing gaps in this file's coverage of untouched lines, not something this change introduced.

Caveat on your reported numbers: running `tests/scripts` from a partial copy produces a collection error in `test_chat_profile_wiring.py` that masks itself as a universal kill. My first mutation pass hit that and reported 18/18 killed spuriously; the table above is from the corrected run scoped to `test_detect_changed_surfaces.py`. The in-repo full suite does pass at 89 passed / 80 subtests, matching what you saw.

**Test meaningfulness.** Four of the five new tests each uniquely kill at least one mutant. `test_directory_glob_still_matches_real_children` is the one that does not — every mutant it catches is also caught by `test_prisma_config_stays_in_the_chat_surface` or the pre-existing `test_path_matches_directory_glob`. It is a reasonable symmetry guard, not a defect.

## Findings

### 1. `import yaml` breaks the `script-tests` job on every PR — 97

`/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/contoso/tests/scripts/test_ci_workflow_wiring.py:5`

The `script-tests` job installs no Python dependencies. Its only step is `make test-scripts PYTHON_BASE=python`, and `make test-scripts` has an order-only prereq on `$(VENV_PYTHON)`, whose recipe is `python -m venv .venv` plus a pip upgrade — nothing else. There is no root-level Python manifest in the repo at all (`git ls-files` finds only the five `services/chat/**` manifests), and every existing `scripts/*.py` and `tests/scripts/*.py` is stdlib-only. PyYAML is present in your local `.venv` purely as a transitive dependency of `prompty`, pulled in by `make setup-chat`:

```
$ .venv/bin/python -m pip show pyyaml
Version: 6.0.3
Required-by: prompty
```

Reproduced against a clean venv, which is what the CI job builds:

```
ImportError: Failed to import test module: test_ci_workflow_wiring
  File ".../tests/scripts/test_ci_workflow_wiring.py", line 5, in &lt;module&gt;
    import yaml
ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'yaml'
```

`unittest discover` reports that as an error, so `make test-scripts` exits non-zero and the whole suite goes red — not just the four new tests. The gate widening makes this worse rather than better: the job now runs on essentially every PR, so this fails every PR. It passes locally and in `full-ci-main`/`onboarding-smoke` only because those paths run chat setup first, which is exactly the seam that hides it.

Fix: drop the YAML dependency. `CiWorkflowWiringTests` in the same file already reads `ci.yml` as text; extract the `script-tests` block's `if:` line with a regex over `read_text()` and assert on the string. Adding `pyyaml` to a manifest is the wrong direction here — there is no root manifest to add it to, and doing so would mean the guardrail suite can no longer run against a bare venv.

Also worth noting: the `# `on:` parses as the boolean True in YAML 1.1` comment becomes moot once `yaml` goes away.

### 2. `test_guarded_files_would_have_skipped_a_runtime_gated_job` passes against the old gate — 80

`/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/contoso/tests/scripts/test_ci_workflow_wiring.py:49-61`

Its docstring says it "establishes that the gate change is load-bearing," but the body never reads `ci.yml`. It asserts only `classify(path)["runtime"] is False and ["chat"] is True`. Revert `ci.yml` to `needs.changes.outputs.runtime == 'true'` and this test still passes, unchanged. It documents the premise of the bug; it does not verify the fix. `test_script_tests_job_is_not_gated_on_runtime` and `test_guarded_files_reach_the_script_tests_job` are the two that actually fail on a revert.

Second problem with the same assertion: `assertFalse(flags["runtime"])` fails if the classification gets *stricter*. Adding `services/chat/scripts/**` to `RUNTIME_PATTERNS` — the issue's own first suggestion, and defensible on its own terms since `check_dependency_policy.py` is repo tooling — turns this suite red with no defect present. A test that forbids a strictly-safer future change is a maintenance tax.

Fix: either fold the premise into `test_guarded_files_reach_the_script_tests_job` as a comment, or keep it but rename to what it checks (`test_guarded_files_classify_as_chat_not_runtime`) and drop the `assertFalse(flags["runtime"])` half, keeping `assertTrue(flags["chat"])`.

## On your four challenges

**1. Always-run vs. the issue's two suggestions — your call is right, and the cost argument is stronger than you stated.** I classified the last 80 first-parent commits on `main`: 68 of them (85%) already set `runtime=true`. `tests/scripts/**`, `ci.yml`, `Makefile`, `package.json` and the manifests are all runtime patterns, so the old gate was already firing for the large majority of PRs. Always-running adds the job to roughly 2-3 additional PRs per month at ~1 minute of runner time each. The gate was buying almost nothing while costing a correctness hole.

On precedent erosion: this does not weaken change-surface routing, because `script-tests` is categorically different from the other gated jobs. `web-ci`, `chat-ci`, `docs-ci` and `integration-e2e` each assert on one surface; `script-tests` asserts on the *wiring between* surfaces, so its input set is the union of everything, by construction. A gate is only meaningful when the guarded set is a proper subset of the repo. It is not here. The precedent this sets is narrow and correct: cross-surface guards do not get surface gates. `env-contract` and `onboarding-smoke` (still `runtime`-gated, per the issue's closing note) are genuinely different — `env-contract-check` reads an enumerable file set, so a gate there is honest.

**2. `none != 'true'` is the right expression.** `always()` would be actively wrong on two counts: it removes the implicit skip-on-`needs`-failure, so `script-tests` would run with a broken `changes` job, and it defeats the `github.event_name != 'push'` clause's intent by running alongside `full-ci-main`, which already runs `make test-scripts` via `make ci`. Removing the `if:` entirely would double-run on push to `main` for the same reason. An explicit disjunction (`runtime || web || chat || docs || unknown`) is strictly worse than the negation — it is the enumeration you rejected, just relocated into YAML, and it re-opens the hole the moment a sixth flag is added.

On unset-output semantics: if the `changes` job's detector fails to write `none`, the expression is `'' != 'true'` → true → run. That is the fail-safe direction, and moot anyway since a failed `changes` job skips the dependent. The one behaviour you should confirm is intentional: `schedule` sets `none=true` explicitly at ci.yml:71, so the weekly full-profile run still skips `script-tests`. That matches the old behaviour (`runtime=false` there) so nothing regressed, but it is the only case where `none` is true in practice — the expression is doing real work exactly once.

**3. The comment is over-long, but it is not a tombstone.** The first three lines explain current behaviour and are the kind of rationale this repo already accepts — `scripts/detect_changed_surfaces.py:39-45` and `:64-74` carry comparable "why this pattern exists" blocks, some with the same historical framing. Where it slips is the middle ("Enumerating the guarded set in `RUNTIME_PATTERNS` would mean...", a rejected-alternative record) and the second paragraph, which states a general GitHub Actions fact that is not specific to this line and reads as argument aimed at a reviewer. I would cut to roughly: *this suite asserts on files across every surface, so any surface gate lets a guarded file change while the guard sits out; `none` is the only flag that is not a surface.* Below my reporting bar — you asked, so I answered, but I would not block on it.

**4. Covered in finding 2.** `test_guarded_files_exist` earns its place (it does prevent the other two from passing vacuously on typo'd paths). `test_guarded_files_reach_the_script_tests_job` is the genuine end-to-end test, though note its `assertIn("none != 'true'", condition)` is loop-invariant — it does not depend on `path` and runs three identical times; hoisting it above the loop would say what it means. Its docstring also oversells slightly: it string-matches the gate rather than evaluating the GitHub expression, so it would not catch a malformed condition that happens to contain that substring.

## Verdict

**Verified — the change is correct and does what it claims, with one real gap in the new tests (not vacuous, but incomplete) and one adjacent inconsistency it leaves behind. No blocking issues found.**

### Findings

**1. Old gate skipped the three chat files / new gate does not (confirmed empirically).**
I classified all three files directly with the detector:
```
services/chat/src/api/chat-entrypoint.sh        → runtime=False, chat=True
services/chat/scripts/check_dependency_policy.py → runtime=False, chat=True
services/chat/Dockerfile                         → runtime=False, chat=True, web=True
```
I then swapped in the pre-change `ci.yml` (from `git show HEAD:...`), ran the new tests against it, confirmed the 4 new tests produce 4 failures (`'runtime' unexpectedly found`, `"none != 'true'" not found`), and restored the working tree byte-for-byte (verified with `diff`). This matches the author's claim.

**2. `none` is emitted on every code path where the `changes` job succeeds.** I traced all four branches in the `changes` job's bash: `workflow_dispatch` (line 60, `none=false`), `schedule` (line 73, `none=true`), empty/zero base SHA (line 94, `none=false`), and the general `detect_changed_surfaces.py` path, whose `classify()`/`write_github_output()` always emit all five flags including `none` on success (verified by reading `scripts/detect_changed_surfaces.py:144-214`). The only way `none` is left unset is if the `changes` job itself fails (e.g. `git diff` raises inside the script, or `make venv` fails) — `set -euo pipefail` makes that fail the step. In that case, per GitHub Actions' documented behavior, a job with `needs:` whose `if:` doesn't call `always()`/`failure()`/`cancelled()` implicitly requires the needed job to have succeeded, so `script-tests` (and every other `needs: changes` job, unchanged by this diff) is skipped outright regardless of what the custom condition text evaluates to. So `'' != 'true'` being `true` is inert in practice — same as the old `'' == 'true'` being `false` was inert. I could not execute a real GitHub Actions run to observe this directly (no runner available in this sandbox); this conclusion rests on documented GH Actions semantics, not an executed test.

**3. No bad interaction with `full-ci-main`.** That job has no `needs: changes` and its `if:` (`event_name == 'push' &amp;&amp; ref == 'refs/heads/main'`) doesn't reference `none`/`runtime` at all. `script-tests` keeps its unchanged `github.event_name != 'push'` clause, so it still never runs on push events — the new `none` clause only takes effect for `pull_request`/`workflow_dispatch`/`schedule`, and for `schedule` it correctly still skips (that path sets `none=true`, `runtime=false`).

**4. The four new tests are meaningful (not vacuous) but leave two mutation-shaped gaps.** I ran two mutations outside the repo (never touching the actual repo file — confirmed via `diff` afterward that `.github/workflows/ci.yml` was byte-identical to the pre-mutation version throughout):
- Mutant A: dropped `github.event_name != 'push' &amp;&amp;` entirely, leaving only `needs.changes.outputs.none != 'true'`. All 4 new tests still pass. Nothing currently asserts the push-exclusion clause survives.
- Mutant B: kept the push clause but AND'd in `&amp;&amp; needs.changes.outputs.docs == 'true'`. All 4 new tests still pass, even though this reintroduces the *exact* original bug — for a chat-only diff touching the three guarded files, `docs` is `false`, so the job would still be skipped.

  The root cause: `test_guarded_files_reach_the_script_tests_job`'s two assertions (`flags["none"] is False`, and the literal substring `"none != 'true'"` appears in the condition text) are checked independently — the test never actually evaluates the GitHub expression against the classify()-derived flags for the guarded files. It verifies necessary conditions, not the end-to-end claim its docstring states. This is a real, reportable gap, not nitpicking — Mutant B is a plausible reviewer mistake (e.g. someone "tightening" the gate) that this suite would wave through.

**5. No other job in `ci.yml` has the same defect.** Checked concretely, not assumed:
- `env-contract` (`make env-contract-check` → `scripts/check_env_contract.py`): reads `config/env_contract.json`, and (from that JSON's `docs_path`/`template_path` fields) `docs/ENV_CONTRACT.md`, `.env.example`, `services/chat/.env.example` — all four are literally listed in `RUNTIME_PATTERNS`. Correctly scoped.
- `onboarding-smoke` (`make bootstrap`): `toolchain-doctor`, `env-contract-check`, `env-init`, `setup` (web `package.json`/`package-lock.json`), `setup-chat` (`constraints.txt`, `requirements-core.txt`, `requirements-local.txt`, `requirements-test.txt`, `requirements-dev.txt`), `sync-web-env`, `agent-doctor` — every file read is enumerated in `RUNTIME_PATTERNS`. Correctly scoped.
- `docs-ci` (`make docs-check`): `verify_docs.py` reads `docs/*.md` + root `README.md`/`AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md`/`GEMINI.md`/`CONTRIBUTING.md`; `check_agent_docs.py` walks the whole repo (skipping `.venv`/`node_modules`/etc.) for `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md`/`GEMINI.md` siblings anywhere, plus the fixed `.github/copilot-instructions.md`. All of those filenames are in `DOC_PATTERNS` as bare filenames, which (per `path_matches`/`Path.match` semantics I confirmed) match at any directory depth, not just root. Correctly scoped.
- `web-ci`/`chat-ci`/`integration-e2e`: self-consistent by construction — their suites live under `apps/web/**`/`services/chat/**`, which are exactly `WEB_PATTERNS`/`CHAT_PATTERNS`.

**6. Adjacent inconsistency the fix leaves behind (out of stated scope, but directly relevant).** `scripts/detect_changed_surfaces.py`'s `recommended_targets()` — used by `make quick-ci-changed`, the table's "default agent loop" local/pre-push check — still gates `test-scripts` on `runtime` only, unchanged by this diff:
```
classify(['services/chat/src/api/chat-entrypoint.sh']) → {'runtime': False, 'chat': True, ...}
recommended_targets(...) → ['quick-ci-chat']   # no test-scripts
```
So a contributor/agent running `make quick-ci-changed` locally before pushing a chat-only change still won't run the guardrail suite, even though CI now correctly will (this output isn't consumed by `ci.yml`'s job gates — those read the flags directly — it only drives local guidance, so it's not a CI-correctness bug, just a stale local recommendation). Worth a follow-up, not a blocker for this PR.

## Finding 1 — stale premise in `docs-check` (confidence 80, low severity)

`/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/contoso/Makefile:224-229`

```make
docs-check: | $(VENV_PYTHON)
	$(PYTHON) scripts/verify_docs.py
	$(MAKE) agent-docs-check
	# AGENTS.md is a docs path, so a change to it never reaches test-scripts.
	# This guard reads AGENTS.md, so it has to run here too.
	$(PYTHON) -m unittest discover -s tests/scripts -p "test_agent_definitions.py"
```

That comment states the exact condition your change removes. I verified both halves:

```
docs/RELEASE.md -&gt; ['test-scripts', 'docs-check']
README.md       -&gt; ['test-scripts', 'docs-check']
```

and the CI gate `none != 'true'` runs `script-tests` for a docs-only PR. So a docs change now reaches `test-scripts` on both paths, the comment is false, and the special-case line is dead weight in every caller (`make ci`, `make release-dry-run`, `quick-ci-changed`, and CI all now run the full suite alongside it).

Not a correctness bug — running a test twice is harmless. The cost is that the comment now teaches the next reader the opposite of the truth, and this is precisely the kind of stale mirror that produced #105 in the first place. Suggested fix: drop the two comment lines and the duplicate `unittest discover` invocation, or, if you want to keep the belt-and-braces run, reword the comment to say why rather than asserting a condition that no longer holds. You may reasonably defer this to a follow-up; it does not block.

## Your specific questions

**Was fixing `recommended_targets()` scope growth?** No, and I'd have flagged it if you had left it. AGENTS.md's "do not combine unrelated work" (line 107) sits in the commit-message step and targets unrelated work. This is the same defect, in the same failure mode, on the second of the two paths that run the guard. The `test-scripts` string in `recommended_targets` was gated on `runtime` for the same reason the CI `if:` was, and #105's title — "Script guardrail tests skip for the chat-only files they guard" — is only half-true after fixing CI alone. Closing it with `make quick-ci-changed` still skipping the guard would mean the pre-push loop and CI disagree, which is exactly the class of drift #89 and #105 both are. The comment at `scripts/detect_changed_surfaces.py:191-194` makes the coupling explicit, which is the right way to carry a deliberate mirror.

The fix itself is correct. I checked the `none` guard specifically: `not flags["none"]` is required, because `recommended_targets` is called with `none=True` on the schedule path and on an empty diff, and without the guard `make quick-ci-changed` would print `test-scripts` instead of "No scoped checks required". The `runtime` branch already contains `test-scripts`, so the placement in the `else` is right and dedup is not doing hidden work.

**Is `evaluate_gate()` justified?** Yes — I tried to kill it and could not. Exact string equality alone is not sufficient, and here is the proof. I mutated the gate back to `runtime == 'true'` *and* updated `EXPECTED_CONDITION` to match, simulating the realistic regression where someone edits both together:

```
FAIL: test_guarded_files_reach_the_script_tests_job (path='services/chat/Dockerfile')
AssertionError: False is not true : services/chat/Dockerfile classifies as
{'runtime': False, 'web': True, 'chat': True, 'docs': False, ...} but the gate skips it
```

Only the evaluator caught it. `EXPECTED_CONDITION` is a mirror of `ci.yml`; a paired edit satisfies the mirror and proves nothing. `evaluate_gate` is the only assertion tied to real classifier output rather than to another copy of the string, so it is the only one that encodes the actual claim in #105. ~30 lines that raise on unsupported syntax rather than defaulting to True is the right shape. `test_gate_evaluator_rejects_a_gate_that_skips_guarded_files` correctly closes the "helper returns True unconditionally" hole.

I also confirmed four gate mutants all fail: dropping the push clause (2 failures), ANDing with `docs == 'true'` (4), ANDing with `chat == 'true'` (1), and swapping to `unknown != 'true'` (1).

**Is the `job_condition()` regex robust?** Robust enough, and it fails loudly in every degenerate case I could construct. Step-level `if:` at six spaces cannot match the four-space anchor; a block scalar (`if: &gt;-`) returns `"&gt;-"` and trips the equality assertion; a wrapped multi-line expression returns a truncated string and trips it too. I confirmed `ci.yml` has no two-space-indented content lines other than top-level keys, so the `^  \S` terminator cannot land inside a `run: |` block. One latent edge: a job that is last in the file has no following `^  \S` and reports the misleading `job 'X' not found`. `full-ci-main` is currently last. That is a failure, not a false pass, and the helper is only called with `script-tests`, so I am not scoring it.

**Are the new `recommended_targets` tests meaningful?** Yes, both kill their mutant. Removing the `test-scripts` clause entirely fails 4 tests; dropping just the `none` guard fails exactly `test_recommended_targets_is_empty_when_nothing_changed`. The two tests are non-overlapping — neither would pass against the pre-change implementation. The `for surface in ("web", "chat", "docs")` loop holds `runtime: False` throughout, which is the part that matters.

The `import yaml` fix is confirmed. I built a venv with only pip and setuptools and ran the full suite: 97 tests, OK, 0.23s. No file under `tests/scripts/` imports anything outside the stdlib.

## Findings

### 1. `chat_can_answer()` inspects an environment that never holds the chat service's config — the strict branch is unreachable in practice (confidence: 92)

`/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/contoso/scripts/e2e_smoke.py:102` reads `os.environ` of the *smoke process*. But `PROJECT_ID` and `DISCOVERY_ENGINE_DATASTORE_ID` reach the chat service through Docker Compose variable substitution from the root `.env` file (`docker-compose.yml:53,58`), and the `e2e-smoke` target (`Makefile:193-209`) never loads `.env` into its own environment before invoking the script.

Verified at the repo root: `.env` sets `PROJECT_ID=local`, yet `os.environ.get("PROJECT_ID")` is `None`.

The repo already establishes the convention for this. `local-provider-check` and `diagnose-chat-local` (`Makefile:91-95`, `99-103`) both do `set -a; . "$(ENV_FILE)"; set +a` precisely because a subprocess needs `.env` values. `e2e-smoke` does not.

Concrete scenario: a developer with real credentials in `.env` runs `make e2e-smoke`. Compose injects the real `PROJECT_ID` and datastore into the chat container, so chat *should* answer for real. Credentials are expired, so it returns `fallback: true`. `chat_can_answer()` reads the shell's environment, sees nothing, returns `False`, prints NOTICE, and the smoke goes green. That is issue #121 verbatim, surviving in the primary documented workflow.

Combined with CI (which sets neither variable on the runner — `PROJECT_ID: local-project` at `ci.yml:227` and `:1012` belongs to the chat-unit-test and full-CI jobs, not `integration-e2e`), `chat_can_answer()` returns `False` in every invocation path that exists in this repo. The `raise` at `:207-210` is dead code unless someone manually exports `E2E_REQUIRE_REAL_CHAT=1`.

Fix: prefer ground truth over inference — `/health/dependencies` already returns `real_chat`, and `check_chat_dependencies` already fetches that endpoint. Failing that, load `.env` in the `e2e-smoke` target the way the two neighbouring targets do.

### 2. The degraded-chat failure is raised as retryable, so it burns the full timeout (confidence: 85)

`check_web_chat_proxy` runs inside `wait_for` (`scripts/e2e_smoke.py:270-275`), which swallows every exception except `NonRetryableSmokeError` and retries until the deadline. The `RuntimeError` at `:208` is therefore retried.

`mock: true` means `REAL_CHAT_AVAILABLE` was `False`, and that is decided at module import time (`services/chat/src/api/main.py:14-18`). It cannot change for the container's lifetime. So the strict branch re-POSTs the chat endpoint every 2s for the full `--timeout` — 300s in CI — before failing with the reason buried in `"failed before timeout. Last error: ..."`.

The file already has the mechanism and a test for it: `check_chat_dependencies` raises `NonRetryableSmokeError` for the unrecoverable local-provider case (`:182`), and `test_wait_for_aborts_immediately_on_non_retryable_error` pins the behaviour. Fix: raise `NonRetryableSmokeError` for the `mock` case at minimum. `fallback` is arguably worth one or two retries during warm-up, but not 150.

### 3. The actual behaviour change in `check_web_chat_proxy` has no test (confidence: 80)

All eight new tests exercise the pure helpers `degraded_reason` and `chat_can_answer`. Nothing asserts that `check_web_chat_proxy` raises on a degraded reply when configured, or that it stays quiet when not — the branch at `:204-219` is the entire point of the change and is uncovered.

This is not a generic coverage note: the file already has the exact pattern (`test_check_web_chat_proxy_fails_without_answer` patches `request_json`), and writing this test would have forced a decision about how to control `os.environ` inside it — which is what would have surfaced Finding 1.

## Answers to your four questions

**1. Conditional failure — right shape, wrong axis.** I would not add credentials to a PR-triggered job, and I would not make degraded unconditionally fatal as written. But the split you chose is on "is the environment configured", when the useful split is "is this degradation credential-dependent":

- `mock: true` means `contoso_chat.chat_request` failed to import. Detecting that needs **zero credentials**. It is exactly the class of regression that `ab1fdaa fix(chat): declare pydantic` was about. By your own analysis CI hits `fallback`, not `mock` — so making `mock` unconditionally fatal would keep CI green today while giving it real regression detection on every PR.
- `fallback` is the only one that genuinely depends on credentials, and it is the only one that needs the conditional.

As written, the strict branch never executes in CI (and, per Finding 1, nowhere else either), so yes — right now it reads closer to a euphemism than a fix. Splitting the two labels resolves that without secrets and without a stub backend.

**2. Inference vs. explicit opt-in.** Finding 1 is the argument against inference, in the strongest form: it inferred "unconfigured" from an environment that had no relationship to the process under test. But explicit-flag-only has its own problem — the default is what CI and every developer actually get, so you have just moved the decision, not eliminated it. The better move is to stop guessing at intent and read the fact: `/health/dependencies` reports `real_chat` from the process under test. Nothing currently asserts its value (the chat unit tests only check the key exists, `services/chat/tests/unit/test_main.py:23,32`). Keep `E2E_REQUIRE_REAL_CHAT` as the override for the credential-dependent `fallback` case only.

**3. NOTICE is the weakest signal available in this job.** On success, `make e2e-smoke` dumps compose logs only when status is non-zero, so the NOTICE just scrolls past in the raw step log. This same job already writes to `$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY` in four places (`ci.yml:352`, `419`, and the full-profile equivalents). A one-line summary entry is the consistent choice and costs nothing. Separately, the `print` sits inside a retried check — benign today because nothing raises before it on that path, but it would repeat once per retry if a retryable raise is ever added above it.

**4. Keep `test_degraded_replies_still_satisfy_the_old_check`.** It earns its place as a characterization test. Issue #121's own suggested fix was to tighten `response_has_answer` itself; if someone later does that, this test fails and tells them the two mechanisms now overlap. It is also the only place the *reason* for the new code is executable rather than prose.

The weaker one is `test_degraded_replies_are_identified` — `assertIn("mock", ...)` against a message containing the word "mock" couples the test to prose and would pass on a message reading "not a mock". Below my reporting threshold, but it is the one to tighten if you touch the class.

## Design change since the task brief was written (important)

The version I first read matched the brief: `chat_can_answer(env)` inferred readiness from `PROJECT_ID`/`DISCOVERY_ENGINE_DATASTORE_ID` in `os.environ`, with `PLACEHOLDER_PROJECT_IDS`. **I independently found this signal was structurally broken** before noticing the file had already been rewritten to fix it: `make -n e2e-smoke` shows the smoke script is invoked as `.venv/bin/python scripts/e2e_smoke.py ...` with no `PROJECT_ID`/`DISCOVERY_ENGINE_DATASTORE_ID` passthrough, and the Makefile never sources `.env` into its own process — those vars reach the `chat` *container* only through Compose's own `.env` substitution (`docker-compose.yml:53,58`). So in a normal local run, `os.environ` in the smoke script would never see them regardless of what `.env` says, making the "configured" branch permanently dead outside CI. Separately, that inference also never accounted for `LLM_PROVIDER=local` (Ollama path), which needs neither var (`services/chat/src/api/contoso_chat/search_service.py:87-97`).

The current version has already replaced this with:
- `require_real_answer(env)` — **explicit-only**, reads `E2E_REQUIRE_REAL_CHAT` and nothing else. Its own docstring cites the exact dead-env-var reason I found independently.
- `degraded_reason()` now returns `("mock"|"fallback", detail)` instead of a bare reason string.
- `check_web_chat_proxy` now treats the two kinds differently: `mock` (chat module failed to import) is **always fatal**, raised as `NonRetryableSmokeError` regardless of credentials, because it needs no credentials to detect and is exactly the "undeclared dependency" regression the issue's own example describes. `fallback` is fatal only when `E2E_REQUIRE_REAL_CHAT` is set; otherwise it goes through a new `report_notice()` that both prints and appends a `&gt; [!WARNING]` block to `$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY` when present, so the caveat survives on a green run instead of scrolling past in the raw log.

Since `chat_can_answer`/`PLACEHOLDER_PROJECT_IDS` no longer exist, the specific question "does the placeholder list/env-var choice match what chat requires, and should REGION be included" is moot — there's nothing left to check there. `grep -rn "chat_can_answer\|PLACEHOLDER_PROJECT_IDS"` across the repo returns nothing, confirming no stale references.

## Behaviour matrix (verified against current code, not the brief's)

| Reply | `E2E_REQUIRE_REAL_CHAT` | Result |
|---|---|---|
| `mock: true` | unset or set | `NonRetryableSmokeError`, immediate (no retry) |
| `fallback: true` | unset (default) | `report_notice(...)`, passes |
| `fallback: true` | `1`/`true`/`yes` | `NonRetryableSmokeError`, immediate |
| real answer (no `mock`/`fallback`) | unset or set | passes |

I confirmed this by direct unit-test reading and by mutation (see below), and confirmed the `fallback`/`no override` row against a real running container.

One behavioral improvement over the brief's stated matrix worth flagging explicitly: the `mock` and `E2E_REQUIRE_REAL_CHAT=1` failure paths now raise `NonRetryableSmokeError`, which `wait_for()` re-raises immediately instead of retrying — so these fail fast rather than burning the full `--timeout` (240s default, 300s/600s in CI) before reporting. The old design (raise a plain `RuntimeError`) would have retried for the whole timeout window on every poll, since `chat_can_answer()`/the underlying misconfiguration doesn't change between polls.

- `scripts/detect_changed_surfaces.py` classifies both `scripts/e2e_smoke.py` and `tests/scripts/**` as `RUNTIME_PATTERNS`, so `runtime=true`, which is exactly the condition (`needs.changes.outputs.runtime == 'true'`) that gates both `integration-e2e` (lite) and would gate `integration-e2e-full-profile` (schedule/dispatch only) — i.e., the real "Integration E2E Smoke" job does run for this change.
- I stood up the actual stack (see Environment section) with `PROJECT_ID=local`, `DISCOVERY_ENGINE_DATASTORE_ID=` (empty), `LLM_PROVIDER=gcp` — the exact values both real CI jobs produce, since neither job sets `PROJECT_ID`/`DISCOVERY_ENGINE_DATASTORE_ID`/`LLM_PROVIDER` and there is no `.env` in the CI checkout (`.env` is gitignored). ## Whether the new tests are meaningful or vacuous — mutation results

Ran mutation testing against a scratch copy of the repo (never modified the actual repo files), using the bare venv.

**Pure functions are well covered.** Inverting `require_real_answer`'s membership test, or its logic, is caught by `test_require_real_answer_is_explicit_only`.

**The integration wiring inside `check_web_chat_proxy` is also now covered** — this is the one gap I found and then watched get closed mid-session. On an earlier revision of this branch (65/89-line diff), the only tests calling `check_web_chat_proxy` used a clean `{"answer": "ok"}` payload, so the entire raise-vs-notice decision was untested by anything that actually exercises `check_web_chat_proxy`. I confirmed this concretely: inverting the `chat_can_answer()` condition, and separately deleting the entire "raise when configured but degraded" branch, both passed all 16 tests that existed at that point. The current revision adds `DegradedChatProxyBehaviourTests`, whose own docstring states this exact rationale ("asserting only on the pure helpers would leave it untested"). Against the current code I re-ran the same class of mutations:
- Inverting the `require_real_answer()` branch in `check_web_chat_proxy` → 2 errors + 1 failure.
- Deleting the entire "mock is always fatal" branch → 2 failures.
- Deleting the `GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY` write in `report_notice` → 1 failure (`test_notice_reaches_the_step_summary`).

## Missing coverage
- No CI job (as configured, zero `secrets.*` usage, confirmed via `grep -c "secrets\." .github/workflows/ci.yml` → `0`) ever sets `E2E_REQUIRE_REAL_CHAT=1` or has real GCP credentials, so the "raise because a real answer was required" path and the "genuinely working, non-degraded chat reply" path are exercised only by unit tests with a mocked `request_json`, never end-to-end against a running container. This is consistent with the issue's own acknowledged trade-off (no secrets in CI), not a defect in this change, but it's a real gap if anyone assumes the E2E job proves a working chat backend — it currently proves only transport/status codes plus the "import didn't explode" signal.
- The `mock` (import-failure) path is likewise only unit-tested with a mocked `request_json`; I could not reproduce it against a real container without deliberately breaking the image's dependency install, which would have meant modifying the repo/build — out of scope here. The unit + mutation coverage on this path is solid, so I did not treat this as blocking.
- `report_notice`'s message text ends with "Set E2E_REQUIRE_REAL_CHAT=1 where credentials exist" — in an `LLM_PROVIDER=local` dev setup, the relevant blocker is usually a missing/unready Ollama, not "credentials." Not a functional defect (the existing `check_chat_dependencies` / `local_provider_ready` check already fails fast and non-retryably before this code ever runs in that scenario), just a minor wording mismatch for that audience.

Verified afterward that only the unrelated `travel-app-*` containers remain — nothing belonging to the user's other project was touched or stopped.

## Findings

### 1. New docstrings assert coverage claims that are false — `scripts/e2e_smoke.py:201-205, 217-221`, `tests/scripts/test_e2e_smoke.py:94-99, 122-127` (confidence 85)

Both ported checks justify themselves with a claim the repository contradicts:

- `check_chat_root`: *"the only check that would notice the root handler being removed or renamed"* — `/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/contoso/services/chat/tests/unit/test_main.py:14` asserts `message == "Contoso Chat API"`, `version == "1.0.0"`, `status == "running"` via `TestClient`. That is stricter than the new check (exact values vs. key presence) and it runs in CI on every chat change (`Chat Service CI` → `npm run ci:chat`).
- `check_chat_rejects_invalid_payload`: *"nothing would notice the request model losing its validation"* — `test_main.py:162` posts `{"customer_id": "1", "chat_history": "[]"}` and asserts `422`. Identical payload, identical assertion, already running.

The same false framing is repeated in the new test class docstring (`tests/scripts/test_e2e_smoke.py:94-99`) and again at line 122-127.

Scenario where this bites: a maintainer trimming smoke runtime reads "the only check that would notice" and keeps a check whose unique value is actually much narrower; or, conversely, someone deletes the `test_main.py` coverage believing smoke is the only place it lives. Root `AGENTS.md:318-320` ("Only write high-value comments if at all") makes a comment that misstates the repo worse than no comment.

The checks themselves are still worth keeping — they verify the *shipped container* serves those routes, which `TestClient` against an in-process `app` cannot. Fix: state that, e.g. "unit tests cover the handler; this covers the route being reachable on the running image", and drop the "only check" / "nothing would notice" claims from all three docstrings.

### 2. Root `AGENTS.md:150` still advertises the deleted directory (confidence 85)

`- \`services/chat/tests/\`: chat unit and integration tests.` The repo map is now wrong; `git ls-files services/chat/tests/` shows only `conftest.py`, `requirements-test.txt`, and `unit/`. You updated `services/chat/AGENTS.md:12-13` but not the root map, and the root file is the one an agent reads first. Fix: "chat unit tests", optionally with the same `scripts/e2e_smoke.py` pointer you added to the scoped file.

## Answers to your four questions

**1. Deletion vs. wiring it in — deletion was right, and both assertions you were unsure about were worse than worthless.**

- The `customer_id` echo is an *inverted* test. The real path returns `{"question", "answer", "context"}` (`services/chat/src/api/contoso_chat/chat_request.py:89-93`) — no `customer_id`. Only the mock branch (`main.py:157-162`) and the fallback branch (`main.py:168-174`) echo it. So `assert data["customer_id"] == "5"` passes **only when chat is degraded** and fails when chat actually works. Wiring that into the e2e job (option 1) would have produced a job that goes red on a healthy stack and green on a dead one.
- The `chat_history` test asserted only `("answer" in data) or ("response" in data)`, which both failure branches satisfy — the exact vacuity #125 fixed.

Nothing credential-free and non-duplicated was left behind. The 30s ceiling is loosely covered anyway: `request_json` uses a 10s socket timeout, so a chat slower than that surfaces as a retried `URLError` and eventually a `wait_for` timeout.

**2. Blast radius is complete apart from finding 2.** Verified: no remaining reference anywhere to `test-chat-integration`, `test:chat:integration`, `test-integration`, or `tests/integration` (searched `.md`, `.yml`, `.json`, Makefiles, shell, workflows). Both `.PHONY` lines are intact — I set-compared `.PHONY` entries against defined targets in both Makefiles and the difference is empty in both directions. The full guardrail suite passes (114 tests). The staged `git rm` removed the whole directory; nothing orphaned on disk.

Three leftovers I judged below the reporting bar, listed only because you asked for completeness: the now-unused `service_url` session fixture at `services/chat/tests/conftest.py:11-14` (its only consumer was the deleted suite — nothing in `tests/unit/` references it); `requests` in `services/chat/tests/requirements-test.txt:2` (only the deleted suite imported it; it is also in `requirements-core.txt`, so removing it changes nothing installed); and the "What it does" list at `docs/INTEGRATION.md:25-33`, which still enumerates 6 steps and does not mention the two new ones — though it already omitted `check_web_dynamic_page`, so that staleness predates you.

**3. `NonRetryableSmokeError` is safe here, with one caveat.** `make e2e-smoke` runs the script on the host against `http://127.0.0.1:8000`, straight into uvicorn — no proxy, so no 502. And this check runs *after* `check_chat_health` and `check_chat_dependencies` have both returned 200, so routes are mounted; a pre-mount 404 is not reachable. Connection-refused and read-timeout raise `URLError`, not an HTTP status, so they never reach your `status != 422` branch and stay retryable inside `wait_for`. The only residual risk is someone later pointing `--chat-url` at a Cloud Run URL, where a 503 during a scale event would fail the whole run instantly. Cheap hardening if you want it (I scored this ~55, below my reporting bar, so treat it as optional): make only a 2xx non-retryable — that is the "validation is gone" signal — and raise a plain `RuntimeError` for everything else so `wait_for` retries.

**4. Four of the five tests are meaningful; `run_tests.sh` is coherent but should probably go.**

`test_root_endpoint_missing_a_key_fails`, `test_answering_a_malformed_request_is_a_failure`, and `test_validation_failure_is_not_retried` all fail against a plausible wrong implementation; the last is the best of the set because it pins the retry decision (`attempts == 1`) rather than just the exception type. `test_root_endpoint_shape_is_checked` is happy-path only and would not fail if the check were weakened — harmless, but it verifies nothing a regression would break.

`run_tests.sh` is left in a valid state: the removed block was self-contained, `set -e` already made the surviving `if [ $? -eq 0 ]` blocks dead code (pre-existing), and `test_chat_test_runner_uses_venv_interpreter` still passes. But yes — I would delete it by the same argument you used on the integration suite. Nothing invokes it (both Makefiles call `pytest` directly), it duplicates `make -C services/chat test`, and its `pip install -r ...` calls carry no `-c constraints.txt`, so it installs a different dependency set than the sanctioned path. One concrete dependency if you do: `/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/contoso/tests/scripts/test_venv_wiring.py:69` reads the file, so it would have to be removed in the same commit or that guard blows up on a missing path.

## Verdict

**Verified — the change is safe to land, with one small non-blocking loose end.** All checks pass, the one assertion I was asked to stress-test (`check_chat_rejects_invalid_payload`) does get a real 422 from a genuinely running chat container, and I found no dangling references to the deleted paths. ## The eight deleted tests, one by one

Source read from `git show HEAD:services/chat/tests/integration/test_chat.py` (still present in git history under `HEAD`, i.e. before this uncommitted deletion).

1. **`test_health_endpoint`** — Covered. Duplicated by pre-existing `check_chat_health` in `scripts/e2e_smoke.py` and pre-existing `services/chat/tests/unit/test_main.py::test_health_endpoint`. Agree.
2. **`test_dependencies_health_endpoint`** — Covered. Duplicated by pre-existing `check_chat_dependencies` and three pre-existing dependency tests in `test_main.py`. Agree.
3. **`test_root_endpoint`** — Covered, ported to the new `check_chat_root`. I confirmed against a real running container that root returns `{"message","version","status","real_chat"}` and the new check's key list matches. Agree, and now verified live.
4. **`test_create_response_basic`** — I partially disagree with "superseded." `check_web_chat_proxy` (plus the #121 degraded-reply rejection) does exercise a real create-response call end-to-end, and does it more rigorously than the old test in one sense (it detects mock/fallback replies, which the old test explicitly could not — its comment literally said "Check for either real response format or mock response format"). But I found something the author didn't flag: the old test's "real chat response format" branch asserted `data["customer_id"] == "1"`, and production `get_response()` in `services/chat/src/api/contoso_chat/chat_request.py` returns only `{"question","answer","context"}` — it never includes `customer_id`. So that assertion would have raised a `KeyError` against a genuinely working real backend; it only ever passed against the mock/fallback shape. This reinforces the deletion decision (the test was already vacuous for the case it claimed to cover) but it means the "superseded" framing slightly overstates what was actually being verified before. Also worth noting structurally: no check in `e2e_smoke.py` hits chat's `/api/create_response` directly with a valid payload — the happy path is only exercised indirectly through the web proxy. Low risk given `test_main.py` hits it directly with mocks, but it's a real (minor) shape difference from the old suite.
5. **`test_create_response_with_customer`** (customer_id echo, id="5") — Genuinely dropped as an end-to-end/live check. Underlying behavior is covered at the unit level (`test_create_response_mock_mode`, `test_create_response_real_mode` which asserts `mock_get_response.assert_called_once_with("1", ...)`, `test_create_response_error_handling`), all pre-existing and unaffected by this PR. Given point 4 above — production doesn't echo `customer_id` in the real-chat path at all — an e2e check asserting that echo would be testing the mock/fallback shape, not real behavior, so I agree dropping it lost little. Acceptable.
6. **`test_create_response_with_chat_history`** — Same reasoning as #5. The old test never verified `chat_history` actually influenced the answer, just that a response came back; unit tests cover passthrough (`test_create_response_default_values`, the `assert_called_once_with(..., "[]")` in `test_create_response_real_mode`). Acceptable to drop.
7. **`test_create_response_invalid_payload`** — Ported to `check_chat_rejects_invalid_payload`. **Verified against a real running chat container** (see below): POSTing `{"customer_id": "1", "chat_history": "[]"}` (no `question`) returns HTTP 422 with FastAPI's standard `{"detail":[{"type":"missing","loc":["body","question"],...}]}` body. This is because `ChatRequest.question: str` has no default, so pydantic validation runs before the handler and rejects the request unconditionally — independent of `REAL_CHAT_AVAILABLE`. Confirmed correct. Not blocking.
8. **`test_response_time`** — Genuinely dropped, deliberately, with no replacement. `wait_for()`'s overall timeout (`args.timeout`, 240s default / 300s in CI) bounds total polling time across retries until a check *first* passes — it is not a per-request latency ceiling and is far looser (300s vs. the old 30s single-call bound) and structurally different (retry loop, not one call). I agree with the author's reasoning that a hard 30s ceiling is brittle against real LLM latency variance, but want to be explicit: there is now no assertion anywhere in the suite that would catch a single chat call regressing to, say, 25 seconds. That is a real, acknowledged gap, not a false claim.

## Live-container verification (the part most likely to block a real CI run)

Per your instructions I did not modify the repo or your `.env`, and did not touch your existing containers (`travel-app-db-1` on 5432, `next-server` on 3000 were left running). I built a scratch compose override outside the repo (`db→55433`, `chat→58000`, `web→53100`) and started only `db` + `chat` with `LLM_PROVIDER=gcp` (shell-env override, not touching `.env`), since `.env`'s `LLM_PROVIDER=local` would otherwise require Ollama, which isn't installed here.

Against the real container:
- `GET /` → `200 {"message":"Contoso Chat API","version":"1.0.0","status":"running","real_chat":true}` — matches `check_chat_root`'s expected keys exactly.
- `POST /api/create_response` with `{"customer_id":"1","chat_history":"[]"}` (no `question`) → **422**, `{"detail":[{"type":"missing","loc":["body","question"],...}]}`. Matches `check_chat_rejects_invalid_payload`'s expectation exactly — **not a blocking finding**.
- `POST /api/create_response` with a valid payload → 200, `fallback: true` (expected, since no real GCP/Discovery Engine credentials are configured in this scratch environment) — consistent with `degraded_reason()`'s handling in `check_web_chat_proxy`.
- I also imported `scripts/e2e_smoke.py` directly and called `check_chat_root(...)` and `check_chat_rejects_invalid_payload(...)` against the live container — both passed.

## Findings

### 1. `pendingIds` is populated for sends that never happen — `apps/web/src/components/chat.tsx:99` (confidence 80)

`sendMessage` registers the id before the empty-message guard:

```ts
const pendingId = `pending-${Date.now()}-${Math.random().toString(36).slice(2)}`;
pendingIds.current.add(pendingId);   // line 99
// ... showPlaceholder / settle / settleWithError / send defined ...
if (message === "") return;          // line 148
dispatch({ type: "add", payload: newTurn });
send(sendChatMessage(newTurn, customerId));
```

Scenario: the user sends a message, the input clears, and they press Enter again (or click the send button) with the field empty. `sendMessage` runs, adds a fresh id to the set, then returns at line 148. No request is issued, so `settle` is never called for that id and nothing ever deletes it. Only `reset()` clears it. Under the old code the equivalent `let settled = false` was a local that was collected immediately; the set is component-lifetime, so this now accumulates.

This is the leak path — not the never-settling-promise one you asked about in question 3. That one is not real: `sendChatMessage` catches internally and always resolves, and even a rejection routes through `.catch(settleWithError)` → `settle` → `delete`. Every actual send is removed.

Memory impact is negligible (tens of ~40-byte strings per session). The reason to fix it is the invariant: `pendingIds` is introduced as "sends whose replies this thread still wants", and it now also contains ids for sends that were never made. Any future reader — "is anything in flight?" to disable reset, gate a spinner, or warn on navigate — gets a wrong answer. The comment on line 70 is already untrue as written.

Fix: move `if (message === "") return;` to the top of `sendMessage`, before the `pendingId` is minted. Nothing between line 82 and 148 has an effect that the early return depends on.

---

## Answers to your four questions

**1. `useRef&lt;Set&gt;` vs `AbortController`.** The ref-set is the right mechanism *for this fix*, and a real abort is a follow-up, not a scope expansion to make here.

The bug in #113 is a UI-ownership bug: a reply landing in a thread that no longer wants it. Ownership is exactly what the set models, and it stays correct even if abort lands later — you would still need the set, because an abort that fires after the response is already in flight does not reliably prevent the `.then` from running.

A real abort would be a wider change than it looks. `sendChatMessage` takes no signal, so you would have to plumb one through `apps/web/src/lib/messaging.ts`. More importantly, its `catch` is unconditional: it logs `console.error("Error sending chat message:", error)` and returns the "Sorry, something went wrong" turn. Add abort without teaching that catch about `AbortError` and every reset produces a console error, and the aborted request resolves with a user-facing error turn that the component then drops — noisier than today. Doing it properly means a signal parameter, an `AbortError` branch in `messaging.ts`, and probably tests on both. That is its own slice.

The cost of not aborting is a wasted LLM round trip per mid-flight reset. Worth an issue for the token spend; not worth blocking this.

**2. Behavioural change from `settled` → set membership.** I traced every path and found no regression.

- Fast reply (before 400ms): `settle` runs, `clearTimeout` kills the timer, `delete` returns true, `resolve` dispatches, reducer's `findIndex` returns -1, appends. Identical to before. Covered by the existing first test, still green.
- `settleWithError`: it calls `settle(timer)(errorTurn)` after logging. Post-reset, `delete` returns false and the error turn is dropped instead of appended. That is the correct new behaviour, and it also means a rejected request after a reset no longer plants "Sorry, something went wrong" in a cleared thread — a second bug the change fixes silently.
- No orphaned spinner is possible. An id leaves the set only via `settle` (which dispatches `resolve`, replacing the placeholder) or via `reset` (which clears all turns). There is no path where the id is gone but a placeholder remains on screen.
- One strictly-better change you did not mention: `delete` is now idempotent, so a double `settle` (for instance if the `dispatch` inside `settle` threw and `.catch` re-entered via `settleWithError`) dispatches once instead of twice.

**3. Stale closure.** None. `sendMessage` closes over `pendingIds` (the stable ref object), not over `pendingIds.current`, so the `has`/`delete` in the timer and promise callbacks always read the live set. `.current` is only touched in event handlers and async callbacks, never during render, which is what `apps/web/AGENTS.md` requires of refs. Under StrictMode the double render discards the second `new Set()` argument and keeps the first instance — correct.

**4. Deferring the reset button to #112.** Deferring is right. Making the reset control a real `&lt;button&gt;` with an accessible name is a visible UI and a11y change that wants the rendered `ui-review` pass step 6 of the root `AGENTS.md` mandates; folding it in here would turn a behavioural bug fix into a mixed diff and make the "does this test fail against the old component?" question harder to answer cleanly. The thin-vertical-slice rule in step 3 points the same way.

The cost you are accepting is that `clickReset()` is coupled to the Tailwind classes, so #112 has to update this helper. Your comment says exactly that, which is the right mitigation. The selector also fails loudly rather than silently — `.w-5.stroke-zinc-500` does not match the send icon (`w-6`), and if it stopped matching the helper throws its own error rather than clicking the wrong thing.

## Failures

## Flakes

None observed. Not re-run for flake detection since nothing failed.

## Mutation testing results (the three requested mutations)

| Mutation | Result | Detail |
|---|---|---|
| Remove `pendingIds.current.clear()` from `reset()` | **Killed** | Both new tests fail: `drops a reply that arrives after the thread was reset` and `still delivers a reply sent after a reset` |
| Change `settle`'s `if (!pendingIds.current.delete(pendingId)) return;` to `if (!pendingIds.current.has(pendingId)) return;` (drop the deletion, keep only the check) | **Survived — all 8 tests pass** | Real finding, see below |
| Remove the membership check in the placeholder timer (`if (!pendingIds.current.has(pendingId)) return;` inside `showPlaceholder`) | **Killed** | `drops a reply that arrives after the thread was reset` fails (placeholder renders into the cleared thread) |

**On the surviving mutation (`delete` → `has`):** I traced why no test catches it. Each `pendingId` is unique per send (`Date.now()` + random suffix), and `settle` is only ever invoked once per send (a promise settles once). `clearTimeout(timer)` already prevents that send's own placeholder timer from firing once `settle` runs, independent of `delete` vs `has`. So leaving the id in the set after settling has no effect on any other in-flight send's membership check — there's no cross-contamination possible with unique ids. The observable difference is that `pendingIds.current` would grow by one entry for every message ever sent in a session that never calls `reset()`, since nothing besides `clear()` would ever remove a settled id. That's a real but minor memory-retention concern (unbounded `Set` growth over a long-lived chat panel, which is mounted once in `src/app/layout.tsx` and persists for the app's lifetime), not a functional/correctness bug — no wrong answer or stuck placeholder results from it. The code comment ("delete reports whether this send was still wanted") frames `delete`'s return value as load-bearing, but the actual load-bearing cleanup (removing settled ids so the set doesn't grow) is untested. Worth a one-line test asserting `pendingIds.current.size` returns to 0 after a normal round trip if you want this covered, though I'd call it low priority given the ids never collide.

## Fast-reply path (explicitly requested)

Verified via the pre-existing `keeps the user message and shows no stuck placeholder when the reply is fast` test (unchanged by this diff, still passing) plus reading the code: `settle` calls `clearTimeout(timer)` unconditionally before checking membership, in both the old (`settled` boolean) and new (`Set`) implementations. That means a placeholder can never appear after a reply lands via the normal path — this was already true pre-fix and remains true. The `Set`-membership check in `showPlaceholder` is a defensive second layer (also present as the `settled` boolean before), not the mechanism actually preventing the fast-reply race; its real new job is reset-awareness. No regression, no behavior change on the fast-reply path — confirmed no placeholder can appear after a reply lands.

## Overlapping sends (#114 regression check)

Both tests — `resolves each reply into its own placeholder when two are on screen` and `...when replies arrive in order` — still pass under the fixed component and still exercise real logic: I confirmed (by reading `chatReducer`'s `resolve` case, unchanged by this diff) that id-based matching plus strict-ordering assertions still distinguish match-by-id from match-by-position/last-waiting-turn. Not affected by this change; still meaningful.

## React concerns

- **Stale closures:** none. `pendingIds` is a ref (`useRef&lt;Set&lt;string&gt;&gt;`), so closures created inside `sendMessage`/`showPlaceholder`/`settle` always see the live, mutable `Set` object rather than a captured snapshot — this is exactly the right tool for cross-async-boundary state that shouldn't trigger re-renders.
- **Refs across re-renders:** `useRef(new Set())` allocates a new `Set` on every render call, but React only keeps the one from the first render; the rest are discarded/GC'd. This is a minor, harmless, common pattern (not flagged by eslint here).
- **Set surviving unmount:** `Chat` is mounted once at `apps/web/src/app/layout.tsx:34` (root layout), so it does not unmount/remount on client-side navigation in practice — the ref living for the app's lifetime is expected, not a leak in the app-router sense. However, note a **pre-existing, unchanged-by-this-diff** gap: there is no cleanup effect that clears outstanding `setTimeout`s or aborts in-flight requests on unmount (e.g., in tests, or in an edge case like a full app teardown). This isn't introduced or worsened by this fix — I flag it only because it's adjacent, not because it's new.

## Missing coverage

- No test asserts that `settle` actually shrinks `pendingIds.current` (the `delete`-vs-`has` gap above) — low priority given ids can't realistically collide.
- No test combines **two overlapping sends together with a reset** (e.g., reset while both placeholders are in flight). The implementation trivially generalizes (`clear()` empties the whole set), but this combined scenario isn't exercised directly.
- No test exercises the reset button through an accessible query — `clickReset()` uses a raw CSS selector because the control has no role/label (tracked as #112 per the code's own comment). Not this fix's problem to solve, but it means keyboard-accessibility of the reset control remains unverified by any test.

None encountered. Dependencies (`node_modules`, Prisma client) were already present; no install was needed. Ports 5432/3000 were not touched — `e2e-smoke` was skipped per your instruction since this is a web-only change and those ports are held by unrelated containers.

## Findings, most severe first

**1. Escape closes the chat out from under the site's own navigation drawer. (High)**
`apps/web/src/components/chat.tsx` — document-level `keydown`. At 390×844 with the chat open, opening the hamburger drawer and pressing Escape leaves the drawer open and silently closes the chat underneath it. Escape dismissed the layer the user was *not* interacting with. Evidence: `phone-M1-menu-open.png` → `phone-M2-after-escape.png`. The comment justifying the document binding ("focus may legitimately sit outside it") is exactly what causes this. Fix: ignore the key when the event target is outside the panel and the panel is not the topmost layer, or bind to the panel and accept that Escape only works with focus inside it.

**2. Focus is dropped to `&lt;body&gt;` when the panel closes. (High)**
Measured `document.activeElement` after Escape at all three viewports: `body`. The next Tab restarts at the top of the document — 24 stops back to the launcher. WCAG 2.4.3. Since focus is now deliberately moved *in* on open, returning it to the launcher on close is the matching half. (I measured the Escape path; the close-button path lands on the launcher naturally since it is the same element.)

**3. Tab leaves the "dialog" into 18 fully obscured controls at 390px. (High)**
With the panel open at 390×844, I walked the 23 page controls behind it and tested each with `elementFromPoint` at its centre: **18 of 23 are completely covered by the panel while focused**, on-screen but invisible (TrailMaster X4 Tent, Alpine Explorer Tent, SkyView 2-Person Tent, Adventurer Pro Backpack, SummitClimber Backpack, …). WCAG 2.4.11 Focus Not Obscured (Minimum), AA in WCAG 2.2. `role="dialog"` now sets the expectation that focus is contained; at phone width the panel occupies 358 of 390 px and 748 of 844 px, so there is nothing useful to Tab to anyway. Fix: trap focus while open at phone width, or add `aria-modal` plus `inert` on the page content.

**4. The new placeholder measures 2.64:1. (Medium)**
`::placeholder` resolves to `oklab(0.37 … / 0.5)` → flattened `rgb(159,159,162)` on white = **2.64:1** at 16px. Below the 4.5:1 body minimum. It matters more than usual here because the field's name is an `aria-label`, so the placeholder is the *only* visible description of the field. Visible in `zoom-bottom-send-focused.png`. Fix: drop the alpha, or use a `text-zinc-500`-equivalent placeholder colour.

**5. Clearing the conversation is silent and lands in an undefined empty state. (Medium)**
`role="log"` announces additions, not removals, so a screen-reader user gets no confirmation. Visually the panel goes completely blank — no greeting, no empty-state copy, `turnsAfterReset: 0` (`phone-10-after-reset.png`). The greeting only re-renders on *open* when `turns.length === 0`, so it does not come back. This state was effectively unreachable before, because the control was not a control; making the button real makes it reachable. Fix: re-seed the greeting on reset, and/or announce "Conversation cleared" via a `role="status"`.

**6. Whitespace-only send is a silent no-op. (Low)**
Guard works — `turnsBefore: 1, turnsAfter: 1` at all three viewports. But the input keeps `"   "` and nothing tells the user why Enter did nothing. Consider disabling send when `message.trim() === ""` so the state is visible rather than inferred.

**7. Bubble gutters were not made responsive along with the panel. (Low–Medium)**
`turn.tsx` keeps `ml-24` / `mr-24` (96px) plus `[&amp;_li]:ml-9` (36px). At 390px that leaves a **218px content column inside a 358px panel — 39% is fixed gutter**, and product names wrap to two lines ("Summit Breeze Jacket" 120.5px over 46px of height). Nothing is clipped, so this is comfort not correctness, but the panel fix would pay off more with `ml-8 sm:ml-24` / `mr-8 sm:mr-24`.

**8. The spinner ignores `prefers-reduced-motion`. (Low)**
Under `reducedMotion: reduce`: `animationName: "spin", animationDuration: "1s", animationPlayState: "running"`. Pre-existing, but the diff touches this exact element, so it is cheap to add `motion-reduce:animate-none` while you are here.

**9. Nested live regions. (Low, verify with a real screen reader)**
`role="status"` (implicitly `aria-live=polite`, `aria-atomic=true`) sits inside `role="log" aria-live="polite"`. Per spec the innermost region owns the change, so this should be correct, but the placeholder→reply swap crosses the boundary and could double-announce on some AT.

### Out of scope, observed while measuring
- The home page still has **zero** `h1`–`h6` (issue #112, comment item 2). Unchanged by this diff.
- Page-level horizontal overflow at 390px: `scrollWidth 476` vs `clientWidth 390`, both with the chat open and closed. Traced to product grid cards (`TrailLite Daypack` right edge 472), **not** the chat panel.
- Issue #112 item 6 ("literal alt text in `turn.tsx`") is **stale** — there is no `&lt;img&gt;` or `alt` in `apps/web/src/components/turn.tsx` at all. Nothing to fix; close that item.

## Checks I could not perform, and why

- **Real chat service replies.** `LLM_PROVIDER=local` with no Ollama on this machine, and no GCP credentials, so `services/chat` cannot start. I stood up a stub on port 8099 returning the documented `{answer, context[]}` shape with real seeded product names, which exercises the full `sendChatMessage` → `extractProductLinks` → `appendProductLinks` → markdown render path. So the rendering, wrapping and link measurements are real; what I did **not** verify is how the actual model's answer formatting (very long answers, tables, code blocks, emoji) lays out at 390px.
- **Actual screen-reader announcement.** No AT available in this environment. I verified ARIA structure, roles, live-region nesting and `aria-hidden` placement — not what NVDA/VoiceOver actually says.
- **Real touch input.** Tap targets are CSS box measurements from `getBoundingClientRect`, not physical touch testing.
- **Classic (non-overlay) scrollbars.** Headless Chromium uses overlay scrollbars, so `100vw` equalled the client width. On a desktop browser with classic scrollbars, `w-[min(650px,calc(100vw-2rem))]` includes the scrollbar in `100vw`, which would leave the panel roughly 1px from the left edge in a ~390px-wide *window*. Theoretical; I could not reproduce it here.
- **Authenticated journey.** All measurements were as a guest ("Hi there, how can I be helpful today?"). A signed-in user's longer display name could change bubble wrapping at 390px; not tested.</result>
