## Findings

### 1. `w-[min(650px,calc(100vw-2rem))]` clips off the left edge again at 640–698px viewports — 85

`/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/contoso/apps/web/src/components/chat.tsx:220` and `:230-232`

The width formula reserves `2rem` of horizontal margin, but the container switches to `sm:mr-12` (3rem) at the same breakpoint where the formula stays at `2rem`.

- vw = 660: `sm` applies, right margin 48px, width `min(650, 660-32)` = 628px. Right edge 612, left edge **-16px**.
- The panel hangs off the left for every viewport in **[640px, 698px)**; with a desktop scrollbar `100vw` includes it, so up to ~31px.

The review that produced this issue measured 390, 834 and 1440 — all three are outside the broken band, so nothing would catch it. Below `sm` (`mr-4`, 16px) the math is right: left edge lands at exactly 16px.

Fix: make the subtraction track the margin, e.g. `w-[min(650px,calc(100vw-2rem))] sm:w-[min(650px,calc(100vw-6rem))]`, or drop the horizontal margin bump and keep `mr-4` at all widths.

### 2. `reset()` within 400ms of opening an empty panel renders the greeting twice — 85

`/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/contoso/apps/web/src/components/chat.tsx:108-117` against `:208-215`

`toggleChat` queues an unguarded, untracked 400ms `setTimeout` that dispatches `greeting()` whenever the panel opens with `turns.length === 0`. `reset()` now also dispatches a greeting, and nothing cancels the pending timer.

Scenario: open the chat, click "Clear conversation" before 400ms elapse. Reducer state goes `[] → [greeting]` (reset), then `[greeting, greeting]` (timer). Confirmed by probe against the working tree — two matches for `/how can I be helpful today/i`. Your existing test `drops a reply that arrives after the thread was reset` already produces this state; it just never asserts on the greeting.

Note the two dispatches themselves are sound: React 19 batches them and the reducer applies `clear` then `add` in order, so `[greeting]` is correct. The defect is the orphan timer, not batching.

Fix: hold the timeout id in a ref, `clearTimeout` it in `reset()` (and arguably in `sendMessage`, which would also fix the pre-existing case where typing within 400ms of opening lands the greeting *after* the user's first message).

### 3. Escape does nothing once focus has fallen to `&lt;body&gt;` from a click inside the panel — 80

`/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/contoso/apps/web/src/components/chat.tsx:72`

`if (!panelRef.current?.contains(event.target as Node)) return;` is the right scoping decision — the sidebar (`sidebar.tsx`, `z-50`, no Escape handler of its own) does sit over the chat at `z-10`, so an unscoped listener really would dismiss the wrong layer. But it leaves the case you asked about.

Clicking a non-focusable region of the panel — a message bubble, the header strip, the padding around the log — blurs the input and makes `document.body` the active element. `event.target` is then `&lt;body&gt;`, `contains()` is false, and Escape is silently ignored. Probe confirms: after `blur()`, `keyDown Escape` leaves the dialog mounted.

A panel-bound React `onKeyDown` has exactly the same hole, so this is not an argument against your design. Fix: add `tabIndex={-1}` to the panel div. Browsers focus the nearest focusable ancestor on mousedown, so clicks on non-focusable descendants land on the panel itself, `event.target` stays inside, and the tab order is unaffected. The focus-on-open effect still puts focus in the input.

### 4. The focus-return fix in `toggleChat` is unverified; the test named for it only covers Escape — 80

`/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/contoso/apps/web/src/components/chat.tsx:207` vs `/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/contoso/apps/web/src/components/chat.test.tsx:120-127`

`returns focus to the launcher when closed` closes with Escape, which is served by the handler at `chat.tsx:74` — a different line from the `if (showChat) launcherRef.current?.focus();` added to `toggleChat`. Delete line 207 and all 20 tests still pass, yet the click/tap close path regresses in Safari (macOS Safari does not focus buttons on click, so focus would sit on the unmounted input and fall to `&lt;body&gt;`).

A test would discriminate: probe confirms jsdom's `fireEvent.click` leaves `activeElement` as `BODY`, and with line 207 present, clicking "Close chat" ends with `activeElement` = the launcher. Add three lines closing via the launcher rather than Escape.

## Answers to what you asked me to challenge

**1. `role="dialog"` without `aria-modal` or a trap — keep it.** Firm recommendation: ship the role, do not add `aria-modal`, do not add a trap. `role="dialog"` is not a claim of containment; containment is what `aria-modal="true"` claims. The ARIA APG has a distinct non-modal dialog pattern whose obligations are exactly the four you implemented: accessible name, focus moves in on open, Escape closes, focus returns to the trigger. You are honouring the semantics you assert. Dropping the role would cost the accessible name and the grouping boundary and would not move WCAG 2.4.11 one inch, because that finding is about visual layering, not about the role — the 18 controls are obscured whether or not the element announces itself as a dialog. If you decide to close 2.4.11 later, the correct instrument is modal-below-`sm`: scrim, `inert` on the page content, `aria-modal="true"`, trap — all four together, at that breakpoint only. Not a blocker on this commit; the current state is strictly better than `main`.

**2. Scoped Escape.** Correct in intent, one dead spot — finding 3 above.

**3. Two dispatches.** Batching is fine; the timer is not — finding 2 above.

**4. Other vacuous tests.** I walked all ten new tests against the pre-change implementation. Nine fail for the right reason (no accessible names, no `role=log`, no `role=status`, no focus-on-open, no `dialog` role, untrimmed send). The re-seed test's drain is correct and now non-vacuous. I agree the Escape-scoping test guards a regression rather than discriminating your design; that is a reasonable thing for it to do. The only real gap is finding 4. Mock implementations survive `vi.clearAllMocks()` across the new block, but no test in it depends on a leaked implementation, so nothing is vacuous by that route.

**5. `turn.tsx`.** No findings. `role="status"` nested inside the `role="log"` region is redundant — the log announces the insertion regardless — but it is harmless, matches what the issue asked for, and preserves the announcement if the log container is ever changed. `aria-hidden` on the spinner is right since the adjacent text carries the meaning, and that text is what survives `motion-reduce:animate-none`, so the reduced-motion state still communicates work in progress. Gutters and `[&amp;_li]:ml-5` leave room for the outside marker at the narrow width.

Note the margin is only 0.33 — zinc-500 on pure white is right at the edge; if the input ever gains a tint it drops below.

## New issues introduced

**A. (Medium) Escape scoping over-corrected — two dead spots.** `/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/contoso/apps/web/src/components/chat.tsx:72`

```js
if (!panelRef.current?.contains(event.target as Node)) return;
```

Clicking the transcript (a non-focusable div) sets `activeElement` to `BODY`, so the keydown target is `body`, not inside the panel — **Escape is then ignored**. Reproduced at 390×844 *and* 1440×900. This is the ordinary path: read the reply, tap/click the transcript, press Escape. Same for focus sitting on the launcher itself. Suggest widening the guard rather than narrowing it: dismiss when the target is inside the panel **or** is `document.body`/the launcher, and bail only when the target sits inside a higher overlay.

**B. (Medium) Long unbroken token overflows the panel at 390px.** `/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/contoso/apps/web/src/components/turn.tsx` — bubble is a `grow` flex child with no `min-w-0` and no `break-words`. A 63-char unbroken string produced a **590px bubble inside the 358px panel (+272px past the right edge)**; the log gained horizontal scroll (scrollWidth 658 vs clientWidth 358) and the assistant bubble was clipped on the *left* so `https://` was cut off. Screenshot `state-longtoken.png`. Realistic trigger: a pasted URL or order number, or a long slug in an answer. This was unreachable before only because the 650px panel sat off-screen at this width, so the width fix newly exposes it. Add `min-w-0` to the flex row and `break-words` to the bubble.

**C. (Low) Double greeting** — item 4 above.

## On item 8: I think you are right, don't drop `role="dialog"`

Keep it. `role="dialog"` **without** `aria-modal="true"` *is* a non-modal dialog, and the ARIA APG only requires focus containment for modal dialogs. I confirmed the panel renders `aria-modal: null` — so it is currently claiming exactly the semantics it honours. Dropping the role would lose the accessible name ("Chat with Jane Doe") and the boundary announcement, which is a net loss for no correctness gain.

That said, the measured reality justifies the follow-up being real work, not a nicety. Full Tab cycle at 390×844 with chat open: **26 of 43 focus stops land outside the widget on controls fully covered by the panel** (`elementFromPoint` at their centre returns the chat panel) — every product link on the home page. At 1440×900 the panel covers a corner and most stops stay visible. That asymmetry is the argument *for* your framing: the right fix is "modal below `sm`" (`aria-modal` + `inert` on the page + trap, phone width only), which matches what the panel actually looks like at each size. I'd file it as Medium-High and not let it drift.

Items 9 and 10: agree with deferring both. On 9, I confirmed the nesting structurally (`role="status"` inside `role="log"`/polite) but **could not verify whether it actually double-announces — no screen reader is available in this environment**. On 10, confirmed the silent no-op: turns before 3, after 3, and the input retains `"   "` with no feedback.

## Issue #115 (home page overflow) — not made worse

Measured at 390×844 both ways: scrollWidth **476** vs clientWidth **390** (+86px) with chat **closed**, and **476 vs 390** (+86px) with chat **open**. The panel contributes **0px**. No overflow at 834 or 1440. This diff does not touch it.

### Finding 1 (High) — duplicate greeting survives on close-then-reopen inside the 400ms window

`reset` now cancels the pending timer, but `toggleChat`'s **close** path does not. Close and reopen before the timer fires and you get two greetings.

| close at | reopen at | greetings |
|---|---|---|
| +50ms | +100ms | **2** |
| +100ms | +200ms | **2** |
| +150ms | +300ms | **2** |
| +200ms | +350ms | **2** |
| +300ms | +380ms | **2** |
| +450ms | +600ms | 1 |
| +100ms | +600ms | 1 |

Evidence: `bug-double-greeting-390x844.png` (two identical "Hi there, how can I be helpful today?" bubbles).

Mechanism, in `/home/scottdensmore/Developer/scottdensmore/contoso/apps/web/src/components/chat.tsx`: the open branch guards on `state.turns.length === 0`, but a timer scheduled by the first open has not committed its dispatch yet, so the reopen still sees length 0 and schedules a second timer. Both fire. This is the same class of bug the reset fix addressed, one door over — an impatient tap-tap-tap on the launcher is a very ordinary mobile gesture.

Suggested fix: clear `greetingTimer.current` in the close branch of `toggleChat` too, or (more robustly) guard on a `hasGreeted` ref rather than on reducer state the pending timer has not yet written.

### Finding 2 (Medium) — `role="dialog"` without `aria-modal` or focus containment

The panel announces itself as a dialog, but content behind it stays in the tab order: Shift+Tab from the panel lands on a product link underneath, and Tab past "Close chat" wraps into the page. At 390×844 the panel covers 358 of 390px width and 748 of 844px height, so a keyboard or screen-reader user reaches links they cannot see. `role="dialog"` is introduced by this change, so this is a new promise the implementation does not keep. Either add `aria-modal="true"` with a focus trap and inert background, or drop `role="dialog"` in favour of a labelled non-modal region — the widget is genuinely non-modal (the launcher stays visible), so the second is likely the smaller change. Escape does provide an escape hatch, which is why this is Medium and not High.

### Finding 3 (Low, pre-existing, not yours) — home page overflows horizontally at 390px

`document.scrollWidth` 476 vs `clientWidth` 390 — 86px of overflow from the product grid (`TrailLite Daypack`, `TrekStar Hiking Sandals`, `Adventure Dining Table` cards extend past the viewport). Identical with the chat closed and open, and **zero** overflowing elements inside the panel, so this is not caused by this change. Flagging only so it is not misattributed to the chat work.
